Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my comprehensive findings:

## Comprehensive Analysis: CCC Indexing and Exclusion Pattern Handling

I've thoroughly analyzed the bmad-module-skill-forge codebase. Here are my detailed findings regarding how the create-skill workflow handles CCC indexing for remote repos:

### **Question 1: Does the workflow configure CCC with brief exclusion patterns BEFORE starting the index process?**

**Answer: NO - Brief exclusion patterns are NOT used to configure CCC itself.** Instead:

1. **CCC Configuration Happens in Setup-Forge (setup-forge/steps-c/step-01b-ccc-index.md, lines 81-114)**
   - CCC is configured with **SKF infrastructure exclusions** (read-only, framework-specific patterns)
   - These patterns are applied to `.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` ONCE during setup, NOT during create-skill
   - Patterns exclude: `**/_bmad`, `**/_bmad-output`, `**/.claude`, `**/_skf-learn`, `**/{skills_output_folder}`, `**/{forge_data_folder}`
   - These are static infrastructure exclusions that apply globally to all skills compiled on that project

2. **Brief Exclusion Patterns Are Different**
   - Brief `exclude_patterns` (and `include_patterns`) are per-skill scope filters
   - They are NOT passed to CCC configuration
   - They are applied ONLY at AST extraction time (step-03), NOT at CCC discovery time (step-02b)

### **Question 2: What is the sequencing between brief loading, exclusion pattern extraction, CCC configuration, and CCC indexing?**

**Answer: The sequencing is:**

**SETUP PHASE (runs once, before any skill compilation):**
```
setup-forge/step-01: Detect tools (ccc availability) 
   ↓
setup-forge/step-01b: CCC Index Verification
   - IF ccc is available:
     - Read/write SKF infrastructure exclusions to .cocoindex_code/settings.yml
     - Create or verify CCC index with these static exclusions
     - Store ccc_index state in forge-tier.yaml
   - IF ccc unavailable: skip silently
   ↓
setup-forge/step-02: Write forge-tier.yaml with ccc index state
```

**CREATE-SKILL PHASE (runs per skill):**
```
create-skill/step-01: Load Brief
   - Load skill-brief.yaml
   - Validate required fields (name, source_repo, scope, language)
   - Brief contains: include_patterns, exclude_patterns (per-skill scope filters)
   - Load forge-tier.yaml (contains ccc_index state from setup-forge)
   ↓
create-skill/step-02b: CCC Semantic Discovery (Forge+/Deep only, if ccc available)
   - Check: Is tier Forge+ or Deep? Is ccc available? Is source local?
   - IF remote source: Defer discovery (set ccc_discovery: [])
   - IF local source AND ccc available:
     - Use CCC index created in setup-forge (with SKF exclusions, NOT brief exclusions)
     - Build query from brief.name + brief.scope ONLY
     - Execute: ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"
     - Store results in ccc_discovery (pre-ranking hints)
   - Note: Brief exclude_patterns are NOT passed to ccc
   ↓
create-skill/step-03: Extract
   - Section 2: Apply Scope Filters
     - Apply brief's include_patterns AND exclude_patterns to filtered file list
     - Record "Filtered file count: {N} files in scope"
   - Section 2a: Resolve Source Access
     - For remote repos: ephemeral clone with sparse-checkout
     - IF remote AND Forge+/Deep AND ccc_discovery was deferred:
       - Run: cd {temp_path} && ccc init (creates fresh index in temp clone)
       - Apply SKF exclusions to .cocoindex_code/settings.yml in temp (hardcoded patterns)
       - Run: ccc index (index the temporary clone)
       - Run: ccc search on temp index (deferred discovery)
       - Still NO brief exclusions passed to ccc
     - Else if ccc_discovery populated from step-02b:
       - Pre-rank filtered file list by CCC relevance scores
   - Section 4: Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
     - For >500 files: Inject brief's scope.exclude patterns into AST-grep CLI
       Python EXCLUDES list (line 150, extraction-patterns.md)
       → Brief exclusions filter results AFTER AST search, before head -N cap
```

---

### **Key Architectural Insights:**

| Aspect | Setup-Forge | Create-Skill Step-02b | Create-Skill Step-03 |
|--------|-------------|----------------------|----------------------|
| **CCC Configuration** | Configures with SKF infrastructure exclusions | Uses pre-configured CCC index | (Remote) Re-indexes temp clone with SKF exclusions |
| **Brief Exclusion Patterns** | N/A | NOT passed to ccc search | Applied to AST extraction AFTER ccc pre-ranking |
| **Timing** | One-time (per project setup) | Per-skill, pre-extraction discovery | Per-skill, during extraction |
| **Remote Repo Handling** | N/A | Defers discovery (set empty) | Creates ephemeral clone, runs ccc init+index+search on temp |

### **Critical Sequencing for Remote Repos:**

From step-03 lines 77-96 (source-resolution-protocols.md):

```
1. Ephemeral clone created with sparse-checkout (optional, based on include/exclude patterns)
2. IF deferred discovery scenario (remote source + Forge+/Deep + ccc_discovery empty):
   a. cd {temp_path} && ccc init (initialize index for clone)
   b. Apply SKF exclusions to settings.yml (hardcoded, NOT brief-based)
   c. ccc index (index the clone)
   d. ccc search (semantic discovery on clone with SKF exclusions)
3. Brief exclude_patterns NOT involved in ccc setup
4. Brief exclude_patterns applied to AST-grep filtering (step-03 section 4, >500 files path)
```

### **File References for Verification:**

- **CCC Bridge Knowledge:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md` (lines 80-108 on indexing lifecycle and exclusion patterns)
- **Setup-Forge CCC Step:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-01b-ccc-index.md` (lines 81-114 on exclusion configuration)
- **Create-Skill Step-02b:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-02b-ccc-discover.md` (full step on discovery without exclusions)
- **Create-Skill Step-03:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` (lines 63-105 on scope filtering and CCC integration)
- **Extraction Patterns:** `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` (lines 140, 180-190 on CLI streaming with EXCLUDES)
```

5	---
6	
7	# Step 2b: CCC Semantic Discovery
8	
9	## STEP GOAL:
10	
11	If tier is Forge+ or Deep AND ccc is available, perform a semantic discovery pass over the source code to identify the most relevant files for the skill being created. Store ranked discovery results in context to pre-rank the file extraction queue in step-03.
12	
13	For Quick and Forge tiers, or when ccc is unavailable, skip silently and proceed.
14	
15	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):
16	
17	### Universal Rules:
18	
19	- 📖 CRITICAL: Read the complete step file before taking any action
20	- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
21	- 🔄 CRITICAL: When loading next step, ensure entire file is read
22	- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread
23	- ✅ YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
24	
25	### Role Reinforcement:
26	
27	- ✅ You are a skill compilation engine performing semantic pre-discovery
28	- ✅ CCC discovery is a hint layer — it guides extraction, it does not replace it
29	- ✅ Discovery failure is graceful degradation, not an error
30	
31	### Step-Specific Rules:
32	
33	- 🎯 Focus ONLY on running ccc semantic search and storing results
34	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to extract exports — that is step-03
35	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to modify brief data or source resolution
36	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to block the workflow if ccc fails
37	- ⏱️ Quick and Forge tiers: skip this step entirely and silently
38	
39	## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
40	
41	- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
42	- 💾 Discovery results stored in context as `{ccc_discovery}`
43	- 📖 Results are consumed by step-03 to pre-rank the file extraction queue
44	- 🚫 Never treat ccc results as verified extractions
45	
46	## CONTEXT BOUNDARIES:
47	
48	- Available: tier, tools.ccc, ccc_index from forge-tier.yaml (loaded in step-01)
49	- Available: brief_data (name, scope, description) from step-01
50	- Available: source_root (resolved source path) from step-01 — note: for remote sources, `source_root` may not be a local path until step-03 performs the ephemeral clone
51	- Focus: Semantic discovery only — no extraction, no compilation
52	- Dependencies: step-02 must have completed
53	
54	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
55	
56	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
57	
58	### 1. Check Tier Eligibility
59	
60	**If tier is Quick or Forge:**
61	
62	Set `{ccc_discovery: []}` in context. Auto-proceed silently. Display no message. Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
63	
64	**If tier is Forge+ or Deep:**
65	
66	Check `tools.ccc` from forge-tier.yaml. If `tools.ccc` is false, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` in context and auto-proceed to section 5.
67	
68	If `tools.ccc` is true, check the remote source guard **before** proceeding to section 2:
69	
70	**Remote source guard:** If `source_root` is a remote URL (GitHub repository not yet cloned locally — ephemeral clone happens in step-03), CCC cannot operate yet. Set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and display: "CCC discovery deferred — remote source will be indexed after ephemeral clone in step-03." Auto-proceed to section 5 (step completion). Step-03 will detect the deferred scenario and run CCC discovery on the ephemeral clone before AST extraction begins.
71	
72	If `source_root` is a local path, continue to section 2.
73	
74	### 2. Check CCC Index State
75	
76	Read `ccc_index` from forge-tier.yaml:
77	
78	- If `ccc_index.status` is `"fresh"` or `"created"`: continue to section 3.
79	- If `ccc_index.status` is `"stale"`: display brief note — "CCC index is stale — discovery results may miss recent changes." Continue to section 3.
80	- If `ccc_index.status` is `"none"` or `"failed"`: attempt lazy indexing via `ccc_bridge.ensure_index(source_root)`. If indexing succeeds, continue to section 3. If indexing fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and auto-proceed to section 5.
81	
82	**Tool resolution for ccc_bridge.ensure_index:** Use `/ccc` skill indexing (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {source_root} && ccc init` + `ccc index` (CLI). Note: `ccc init` takes no positional arguments — it initializes the index for the current working directory. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
83	
84	### 3. Construct Semantic Query
85	
86	Build the discovery query from the brief data:
87	
88	**Primary query:** `"{brief.name} {brief.scope}"`
89	
90	Where:
91	- `brief.name` is the skill name from the brief
92	- `brief.scope` is the scope field (e.g., "Full library", "Public API", or specific module names)
93	
94	**Query length cap:** Truncate to 80 characters if longer — ccc semantic search is sensitive to overly long queries. When truncating, keep the full skill name and trim `brief.scope` from the end. If `brief.scope` is very short (< 10 chars), append terms from `brief.description` to fill the remaining space.
95	
96	### 4. Execute CCC Semantic Search
97	
98	Run `ccc_bridge.search(query, source_root, top_k=20)`:
99	
100	**Tool resolution for ccc_bridge.search:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {source_root} && ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"` (CLI). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory — there is no flag to specify a project directory. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
101	
102	**If search succeeds:**
103	
104	Store results as `{ccc_discovery: [{file, score, snippet}]}` in context.
105	
106	Display brief discovery summary:
107	
108	"**CCC semantic discovery: {N} relevant regions identified across {M} unique files.**"
109	
110	Where:
111	- `{N}` is the total result count
112	- `{M}` is the count of unique file paths in results
113	
114	**If search fails (any error):**
115	
116	Set `{ccc_discovery: []}` in context.
117	
118	Display: "CCC discovery unavailable — proceeding with standard extraction."
119	
120	Do NOT halt. This is not an error.
121	
122	**If search returns empty results:**
123	
124	Set `{ccc_discovery: []}` in context.
125	
126	No message needed — empty results are normal for small or highly focused libraries.
127	
128	### 5. Auto-Proceed
129	
130	Immediately load, read entire file, then execute `{nextStepFile}`.
131	
132	#### EXECUTION RULES:
133	
134	- This is an auto-proceed step with no user choices
135	- Quick/Forge tiers skip directly with no output
136	- Forge+/Deep with ccc displays brief discovery summary then auto-proceeds
137	- CCC failures do not halt — degrade and proceed
138	
139	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
140	
141	ONLY WHEN discovery is complete (Forge+/Deep with ccc) or the step is skipped (Quick/Forge or ccc unavailable) will you proceed to load `{nextStepFile}` for AST extraction.
142	
143	---
144	
145	## 🚨 SYSTEM SUCCESS/FAILURE METRICS
146	
147	### ✅ SUCCESS:
148	
149	- Quick/Forge tiers: skipped silently with no output, auto-proceeded
150	- Forge+/Deep with ccc: semantic query constructed from brief data
151	- Forge+/Deep with ccc: ccc_bridge.search executed, results stored as `{ccc_discovery}` in context
152	- Discovery summary displayed (count of regions and unique files)
153	- CCC failures handled gracefully (empty discovery, continue)
154	- Auto-proceeded to step-03
155	
156	### ❌ SYSTEM FAILURE:
157	
158	- Halting on CCC unavailability or search failures
159	- Extracting exports or signatures (that is step-03)
160	- Displaying skip messages for Quick/Forge tiers
161	- Treating CCC discovery results as verified extractions
162	- Not storing `{ccc_discovery}` in context (even if empty)
163	- Beginning compilation or SKILL.md assembly in this step
164	
165	**Master Rule:** CCC discovery is a hint layer. It improves extraction coverage but never replaces structural verification. Failures degrade gracefully.
166

1	# CCC Bridge
2	
3	## Principle
4	
5	`ccc_bridge.*` references in workflow steps are **conceptual interfaces**, not callable functions. They describe a semantic code discovery operation to perform. Use the `ccc` MCP server tools (when available) or `ccc` CLI commands to execute these operations. See the TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK rule — if ccc is unavailable, the calling step falls back to direct ast-grep or source reading without ccc pre-discovery. For the complete bridge-to-tool resolution table covering all IDE environments, see [tool-resolution.md](tool-resolution.md).
6	
7	## Rationale
8	
9	Without ccc pre-discovery, extraction steps scan all source files uniformly — processing them in directory order or entry-point-first order. On large codebases (500+ files), this means AST extraction in CLI streaming mode uses `head -N` cutoffs that may miss relevant exports in files that appear late in the scan. Integration detection in Stack Skill relies on grep-based co-import counting, which misses semantic relationships between libraries that don't appear in the same file.
10	
11	With ccc pre-discovery:
12	- Extraction steps receive a relevance-ranked file queue — the most semantically important files are processed first, before any streaming cutoff
13	- Integration detection gains semantic augmentation — pairs below the 2-file co-import threshold can be evaluated via natural language queries
14	- Audit workflows can detect renamed/moved exports via semantic search before classifying them as deleted
15	
16	The key architectural constraint: ccc discovers, ast-grep verifies. Discovery method is orthogonal to confidence tier. This keeps the 4-tier confidence system (T1/T1-low/T2/T3) clean and avoids tier proliferation.
17	
18	## When ccc Is Used
19	
20	ccc is a **discovery layer only**. It answers "where should I look?" — it does not produce citations or structural claims. Every path or symbol returned by ccc_bridge must be verified by `ast_bridge` (T1) or source reading (T1-low) before it enters the extraction inventory. ccc results never appear in provenance citations.
21	
22	ccc is **required** at the Forge+ tier (it defines Forge+) and **optionally available** at the Deep tier as an enhancement (when `tools.ccc: true` in forge-tier.yaml).
23	
24	## Availability
25	
26	ccc_bridge operations are available when:
27	- `tools.ccc: true` in forge-tier.yaml (verified by `ccc --help` + `ccc doctor` in setup-forge)
28	- `ccc_index.status` is `"fresh"` or `"stale"` in forge-tier.yaml (an index exists for the project)
29	
30	When either condition is false, calling steps skip ccc discovery silently and proceed with direct ast-grep or source reading. This is standard Forge tier behavior — not a degradation.
31	
32	## Operations
33	
34	### `ccc_bridge.search(query, path?, top_k?)`
35	
36	**Resolves to:** `cd {path} && ccc search --limit {top_k} "{query}"` (CLI) or the `ccc` MCP search tool (preferred). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory — there is no flag to specify a project directory. The `--path` flag is a file path glob filter within the index, not a project selector.
37	
38	Returns: list of `{file, score, snippet}` entries ranked by semantic relevance to the query. These are **candidates** for ast-grep extraction — not verified exports.
39	
40	**Usage context:** Called before ast-grep in Forge+ and Deep tier extraction steps to discover semantically relevant source regions. Results pre-rank the file extraction queue so ast-grep processes the most relevant files first.
41	
42	### `ccc_bridge.ensure_index(path)`
43	
44	**Resolves to:** Check `ccc_index.status` in forge-tier.yaml. If `"none"` or the indexed_path does not match, run `cd {path} && ccc init` then `ccc index` and update forge-tier.yaml. Note: `ccc init` takes no positional arguments — it initializes the index for the current working directory.
45	
46	**Usage context:** Called by setup-forge step-01b to ensure the project root is indexed. Called lazily by extraction steps when `ccc_index.status` is `"none"` but ccc is available.
47	
48	### `ccc_bridge.status()`
49	
50	**Resolves to:** Two-step verification:
51	1. `ccc --help` — confirms binary exists (exit 0)
52	2. `ccc doctor` — confirms daemon is running, extracts version string, validates embedding model
53	
54	**Usage context:** Called exclusively by setup-forge step-01 during tool detection. Downstream workflows read the result from forge-tier.yaml — they do not re-verify.
55	
56	## Confidence
57	
58	ccc discovery does not produce a confidence tier. The provenance chain is:
59	
60	1. ccc discovers candidate files (internal hint — not cited)
61	2. ast-grep verifies exports in those files → **T1** citation `[AST:file:Lnn]`
62	3. Or source reading verifies → **T1-low** citation `[SRC:file:Lnn]`
63	
64	The ccc search is invisible in the output artifact. A Forge+ skill's citations are indistinguishable from a Forge skill's citations — the difference is in extraction coverage, not citation format.
65	
66	## Indexing Lifecycle
67	
68	### When Indexing Happens
69	
70	1. **setup-forge step-01b:** Indexes the project root when setup-forge runs. This is the primary indexing point.
71	2. **Workflow discovery steps:** If `ccc_index.status` is `"stale"` or `"none"`, discovery steps trigger a re-index and warn the user. They do not block.
72	3. **ccc daemon:** Incremental indexing means re-indexing unchanged files is a near-no-op.
73	
74	### Freshness
75	
76	- Staleness threshold: 24 hours (configurable via `ccc_index.staleness_threshold_hours` in forge-tier.yaml)
77	- A stale index still produces useful results — the workflow proceeds with the stale index and notes the staleness
78	- setup-forge is the designated refresh authority
79	
80	### Exclusion Patterns
81	
82	CCC stores its configuration at `{project-root}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml`. This file contains `exclude_patterns` and `include_patterns` arrays in glob format. `ccc init` creates the file with sensible defaults (excludes `node_modules`, `__pycache__`, hidden dirs, etc.).
83	
84	**SKF infrastructure exclusions:** setup-forge step-01b appends SKF-specific exclusion patterns after `ccc init` creates the default config. These patterns prevent indexing of framework and output directories that have zero value for source extraction:
85	
86	| Pattern | Purpose |
87	|---------|---------|
88	| `**/_bmad` | SKF framework module (workflow instructions, agents, knowledge) |
89	| `**/_bmad-output` | Build output artifacts (TODO files, reports) |
90	| `**/.claude` | Claude Code configuration |
91	| `**/_skf-learn` | SKF learning materials |
92	| `**/{skills_output_folder}` | Generated skill files (from manifest, default: `skills`) |
93	| `**/{forge_data_folder}` | Compilation workspace (from manifest, default: `forge-data`) |
94	
95	The `skills_output_folder` and `forge_data_folder` values are read from `_bmad/_config/skf-manifest.yaml` when available, falling back to the defaults `skills` and `forge-data`. Patterns are appended only if not already present — user customizations to `settings.yml` are preserved.
96	
97	The configured exclusion patterns are stored in `ccc_index.exclude_patterns` in forge-tier.yaml for reference.
98	
99	### Deferred Discovery (Remote Sources)
100	
101	For remote repository sources (GitHub URLs), CCC cannot operate during step-02b because no local code exists yet. The ephemeral clone happens in step-03. To provide CCC pre-ranking for remote sources:
102	
103	1. **step-02b:** Detects remote source, sets `{ccc_discovery: []}`, displays deferred message
104	2. **step-03:** After ephemeral clone succeeds, detects the deferred scenario (`tools.ccc == true AND {ccc_discovery} is empty AND ephemeral_clone_active == true AND tier is Forge+/Deep`)
105	3. **step-03:** Runs `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` + `ccc index` on the ephemeral clone (extended timeout or background mode, verified via `ccc status`)
106	4. **step-03:** Executes CCC search and populates `{ccc_discovery}` before AST extraction begins
107	
108	The ephemeral clone index is not registered in `ccc_index_registry` — the clone is deleted after extraction and the CCC daemon's own GC handles orphaned indexes.
109	
110	### Relationship to QMD Registry
111	
112	ccc_index and qmd_collections are **orthogonal**:
113	- `ccc_index` in forge-tier.yaml tracks the persistent source code index (one per project)
114	- `qmd_collections[]` in forge-tier.yaml tracks per-skill workflow artifact collections
115	- ccc indexes source code for semantic search; QMD indexes curated artifacts for temporal/knowledge search
116	- The janitor role for QMD (setup-forge step-03) operates independently of ccc_index
117	
118	## Query Volume Bounds
119	
120	To prevent excessive daemon calls, workflow steps cap ccc queries:
121	- **create-skill extraction:** max 2 queries per skill (discovery + optional scope refinement)
122	- **analyze-source mapping:** max 1 query per qualifying unit
123	- **create-stack-skill integration detection:** max 1 query per library pair
124	- **audit-skill re-index:** max 1 query per export missing from its recorded location
125	
126	## Anti-Patterns
127	
128	- Using ccc_bridge results as citations without ast-grep verification — ccc output is never a provenance citation
129	- Blocking a workflow because ccc is unavailable — ccc is always optional
130	- Running ccc_bridge.ensure_index() without checking ccc_index.status first — unnecessary re-indexing
131	- Passing ccc results directly to the extraction inventory — they are candidates, not extractions
132	- Listing ccc as "unavailable" in reports for Quick/Forge tiers — ccc is a Forge+ capability, not something Quick/Forge tiers are missing
133	- Indexing without configuring SKF exclusions — framework and output directories pollute search results
134	- Skipping CCC discovery for remote sources without deferring to step-03 — remote repos deserve the same pre-ranking as local sources
135	
136	## Related Fragments
137	
138	- [tool-resolution.md](tool-resolution.md) — canonical bridge-to-tool and subprocess-to-tool mapping per IDE
139	- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — Forge+ tier definition and positive framing
140	- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — why ccc does not create a new confidence tier
141	- [qmd-registry.md](qmd-registry.md) — the parallel but separate registry for QMD collections
142	
143	_Source: designed as part of the Forge+ tier integration for cocoindex-code semantic code search_
144	
```

12	# Step 3: Extract

14	## STEP GOAL:

16	To extract all public exports, function signatures, type definitions, and co-import patterns from the source code using tier-appropriate tools, building a complete extraction inventory with confidence-tiered provenance citations.
17	
18	## MANDATORY EXECUTION RULES (READ FIRST):

23	- 🎯 ALWAYS follow the exact instructions in the step file
24	- ⚙️ TOOL/SUBPROCESS FALLBACK: If any instruction references a tool you do not have access to, you MUST still achieve the outcome in your main context thread

31	- ✅ Confidence tier labeling is mandatory for every extraction result

34	
35	- 🎯 Focus ONLY on extracting exports, signatures, types from source code
36	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to compile SKILL.md content — that's step-05
37	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to write any output files — extraction stays in context
38	- 🚫 FORBIDDEN to include content that cannot be cited to a source location

41	## EXECUTION PROTOCOLS:
42	
43	- 🎯 Follow MANDATORY SEQUENCE exactly
44	- 💾 Build extraction inventory in context — do not write files
45	- 📖 Load extraction patterns data file for tier-specific strategy
46	- 🚫 If an export cannot be verified, exclude it — do not guess

49	
50	- Available: brief_data, tier, source_root, file_tree from step-01; ecosystem check outcome from step-02 (proceed/halt decision — no named variable stored)
51	- Focus: Source code extraction and inventory building

54	
55	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
56	
57	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise.
58	
59	### 1. Load Extraction Patterns
60	
61	Load `{extractionPatternsData}` completely. Identify the strategy for the current forge tier.
62	
63	### 2. Apply Scope Filters
64	
65	From the brief, apply scope and pattern filters:
66	
67	- `scope` — determines what to extract (e.g., "all public exports", specific modules)
68	- `include_patterns` — file globs to include (if specified)
69	- `exclude_patterns` — file globs to exclude (if specified)
70	
71	Build the filtered file list from the source tree resolved in step-01. Record the result: "**Filtered file count: {N} files in scope**" — this count is the input to the AST Extraction Protocol decision tree in the extraction patterns data file.
72	
73	### 2a. Resolve Source Access
74	
75	Load `{sourceResolutionData}` completely. Follow the **Remote Source Resolution** protocol for Forge/Deep tiers (ephemeral clone, sparse-checkout, cleanup), the **Source Commit Capture** protocol for all tiers, and the **Version Reconciliation** protocol for all tiers. This ensures source code is accessible regardless of which extraction path is taken below (standard, component-library, or docs-only).
76	
77	**Deferred CCC Discovery (Forge+ and Deep — remote sources only):**
78	
79	If ALL of these conditions are true:
80	- `tools.ccc` is true in forge-tier.yaml
81	- `{ccc_discovery}` is empty (step-02b deferred because source was remote)
82	- `ephemeral_clone_active` is true (clone succeeded in source resolution above)
83	- Tier is Forge+ or Deep
84	
85	Then run CCC indexing and discovery on the ephemeral clone:
86	
87	1. **Index the clone:** Run `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` then `ccc index` with an extended timeout or in background mode — `ccc init` takes no positional arguments and initializes the index for the current working directory. Indexing can take several minutes on large codebases (1000+ files). Use `ccc status` to verify completion — check that `Chunks` and `Files` counts are non-zero. If init fails or indexing fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and continue — this is not an error.
88	
89	2. **Construct semantic query:** Build from brief data: `"{brief.name} {brief.scope}"`. Truncate to 80 characters — keep the full skill name and trim `brief.scope` from the end. If `brief.scope` is very short (< 10 chars), append terms from `brief.description` to fill the remaining space.
90	
91	3. **Execute search:** Run `ccc_bridge.search(query, temp_path, top_k=20)`:
92	   - **Tool resolution:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {temp_path} && ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"` (CLI). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
93	
94	4. **Store results:** If search succeeds, store as `{ccc_discovery: [{file, score, snippet}]}`. Display: "**CCC semantic discovery (post-clone): {N} relevant regions identified across {M} unique files.**"
95	
96	5. **On failure:** Set `{ccc_discovery: []}`. Display: "CCC post-clone discovery unavailable — proceeding with standard extraction." Do NOT halt.
97	
98	**CCC Discovery Integration (Forge+ and Deep with ccc only):**
99	
100	If `{ccc_discovery}` is in context and non-empty (populated by step-02b or deferred discovery above):
101	- Sort the filtered file list by CCC relevance score: files appearing in `{ccc_discovery}` results move to the front of the extraction queue, sorted by their relevance score descending
102	- Files NOT in CCC results remain in the queue after ranked files — they are not excluded, only deprioritized
103	- Display: "**CCC discovery: {N} files pre-ranked by semantic relevance** — extraction will prioritize these first."
104	
105	If `{ccc_discovery}` is empty or not in context: proceed with existing file ordering (no change to current behavior).

108	
109	**If `scope.type: "component-library"` in the brief:**
110	
111	"**Component library detected.** Delegating to specialized extraction strategy for registry-first, props-focused extraction."
112	
113	Load and execute `{componentExtractionStepFile}` completely. When that step completes, it returns control here. Resume at section 5 (Build Extraction Inventory) with the enriched extraction data and `component_catalog[]` from the component extraction step.
114	
115	**Otherwise:** Continue with standard extraction below.
116	
117	### 3. Check for Docs-Only Mode
118	
119	**If `source_type: "docs-only"` in the brief data:**
120	
121	"**Docs-only mode:** No source code to extract. Documentation content will be fetched from `doc_urls` in step-03c."
122	
123	Build an empty extraction inventory with zero exports. Set `extraction_mode: "docs-only"` in context. Auto-proceed through Gate 2 (section 6) — display the empty inventory and note that T3 content will be produced by the doc-fetcher step.
124	
125	**If `source_type: "source"` (default):** Continue with extraction below.
126	
127	### 4. Execute Tier-Dependent Extraction
128	
129	Source resolution, version reconciliation, and CCC discovery were completed in section 2a. Proceed with the tier-specific extraction strategy below.
130	
131	**Quick Tier (No AST tools):**
132	
133	1. Use `gh_bridge.list_tree(owner, repo, branch)` to map source structure (if remote)
134	2. Identify entry points: index files, main exports, public modules
135	3. Use `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, path)` to read each entry point
136	4. Extract from source text: exported function names, parameter lists, return types
137	5. Infer types from JSDoc, docstrings, type annotations
138	6. Confidence: All results T1-low — `[SRC:{file}:L{line}]`
139	
140	**Tool resolution for gh_bridge:** Use `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/git/trees/{branch}?recursive=1` for list_tree, `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{path}` for read_file. If source is local, use direct file listing/reading instead. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
141	
142	**Forge/Forge+/Deep Tier (AST available):**
143	
144	⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Before executing AST extraction, load the **AST Extraction Protocol** section from `{extractionPatternsData}`. Follow the decision tree based on the file count from step-01's file tree. This determines whether to use the MCP tool, scoped YAML rules, or CLI streaming. Never use `ast-grep --json` (without `=stream`) — it loads the entire result set into memory and will fail on large codebases. Always use the explicit `run` subcommand with streaming: `ast-grep run -p '{pattern}' --json=stream`.
145	
146	1. Detect language from brief or file extensions
147	2. Follow the AST Extraction Protocol decision tree from `{extractionPatternsData}`:
148	   - ≤100 files: use `find_code()` MCP tool with `max_results` and `output_format="text"`
149	   - ≤500 files: use `find_code_by_rule()` MCP tool with scoped YAML rules
150	   - >500 files: use CLI `--json=stream` with line-by-line streaming Python — **CRITICAL:** inject the brief's `scope.exclude` patterns into the Python filter's `EXCLUDES` list (use `[]` if absent) so excluded files are discarded before consuming `head -N` slots (see template in extraction patterns data)
151	3. For each export: extract function name, full signature, parameter types, return type, line number
152	4. Use `ast_bridge.detect_co_imports(path, libraries[])` to find integration points
153	5. Build extraction rules YAML data for reproducibility
154	6. Confidence: All results T1 — `[AST:{file}:L{line}]`
155	
156	**Tool resolution for ast_bridge:** Use ast-grep MCP tools (`mcp__ast-grep__find_code`, `mcp__ast-grep__find_code_by_rule`) as specified in the AST Extraction Protocol above, or `ast-grep` CLI. For `detect_co_imports`, use `find_code_by_rule` with a co-import YAML rule scoped to the libraries list. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).
157	
158	**If AST tool is unavailable at Forge/Deep tier** (see `{tierDegradationRulesData}` for full rules):
159	
160	⚠️ **Warn the user explicitly:** "AST tools are unavailable — extraction will use source reading (T1-low). Run [SF] Setup Forge to detect and configure AST tools for T1 confidence."
161	
162	Degrade to Quick tier extraction. Note the degradation reason in context for the evidence report.
163	
164	**For each file — handle failures gracefully:**
165	
166	- If a file cannot be read: log warning, skip file, continue with remaining files
167	- If AST parsing fails on a file: fall back to source reading for that file, continue
168	
169	**Re-export tracing (Forge/Deep only):** After the initial AST scan, check for unresolved public exports from entry points (`__init__.py`, `index.ts`, `lib.rs`). Follow the **Re-Export Tracing** protocol in `{extractionPatternsTracingData}` to resolve them to their definition files.
170	
171	### 4b. Validate Exports Against Package Entry Point
172	
173	After extraction, validate the collected exports against the package's actual public API surface:
174	
175	- **Python:** Read `{source_root}/__init__.py` — extract imports to build the public export list. Compare against AST results:
176	  - In AST but not entry point → mark as internal (exclude from `metadata.json` exports)
177	  - In entry point but not AST → flag as extraction gap (trace via re-export protocol)
178	- **TypeScript/JS:** Read `index.ts`/`index.js` — same comparison logic.
179	- **Rust:** Read `lib.rs` — extract `pub use` items. Same logic. **Go:** Scan for exported (capitalized) identifiers.
180	
181	Use the entry point as the authoritative source for `metadata.json`'s `exports[]` array.
182	
183	**If entry point is missing or unreadable:** Skip validation with a warning.
184	
185	### 4c. Detect and Inventory Scripts/Assets
186	
187	**If `scripts_intent: "none"` AND `assets_intent: "none"` in brief:** Skip this section.
188	
189	After export extraction, scan the source for scripts and assets using the detection patterns in `{extractionPatternsTracingData}`:
190	
191	1. Scan source tree for directories/files matching detection heuristics (scripts/, bin/, tools/, cli/ for scripts; assets/, templates/, schemas/, configs/, examples/ for assets)
192	2. For each candidate: verify existence, check size (flag >500 lines), exclude binaries, compute SHA-256 hash
193	3. Extract purpose from header comments, shebang, README references, or schema fields. Record: file_path, purpose, source_path, language/type, content_hash, confidence (T1-low)
194	
195	Add results to `scripts_inventory[]` and `assets_inventory[]` alongside the existing export inventory.
196	
197	### 5. Build Extraction Inventory
198	
199	Compile all extracted data into a structured inventory:
200	
201	**Per-export entry:**
202	- Function/type name
203	- Full signature with types
204	- Parameters (name, type, required/optional)
205	- Return type
206	- Source file and line number
207	- Provenance citation (`[AST:...]` or `[SRC:...]`)
208	- Confidence tier (T1 or T1-low)
209	
210	**Aggregate counts:**
211	- Total files scanned
212	- Total exports found
213	- Exports by type (functions, types/interfaces, constants)
214	- Confidence breakdown (T1 count, T1-low count)
215	- `top_exports[]` — sorted list of the top 10-20 public API function names by prominence (import frequency or documentation position). This named field is consumed by step-03b for targeted temporal fetching and cache fingerprinting.
216	
217	**Script/asset counts (when detected):**
218	- `scripts_found`: count of scripts detected
219	- `assets_found`: count of assets detected
220	
221	**Co-import patterns (Forge/Deep only):**
222	- Libraries commonly imported alongside extracted exports
223	- Integration point suggestions
224	
225	### 6. Present Extraction Summary (Gate 2)
226	
227	Display the extraction findings for user confirmation:
228	
229	"**Extraction complete.**
230	
231	**Files scanned:** {file_count}
232	**Exports found:** {export_count} ({function_count} functions, {type_count} types, {constant_count} constants)
233	**Confidence:** {t1_count} T1 (AST-verified), {t1_low_count} T1-low (source reading)
234	**Tier used:** {tier}
235	**Co-import patterns:** {pattern_count} detected
236	{if scripts_found > 0: **Scripts detected:** {scripts_found}}
237	{if assets_found > 0: **Assets detected:** {assets_found}}
238	
239	**Top exports:**
240	{list top 10 exports with signatures}
241	
242	{warnings if any files skipped or degraded}
243	
244	Review the extraction summary above. Select an option to continue."
245	
246	### 7. Present MENU OPTIONS
247	
248	Display: "**Extraction Summary — Select an Option:** [C] Continue to compilation"
249	
250	#### EXECUTION RULES:
251	
252	- IF docs-only mode (`extraction_mode: "docs-only"`): Auto-proceed immediately to `{nextStepFile}` — no user interaction required
253	- OTHERWISE: ALWAYS halt and wait for user input after presenting the extraction summary
254	- This is Gate 2 — user must confirm before compilation proceeds (except docs-only mode)
255	- User may ask questions about the extraction results before continuing
256

1	# Source Resolution Protocols
2	
3	## Remote Source Resolution (Forge/Deep only)
4	
5	If `source_repo` is a local path: proceed with the tier-appropriate strategy as normal.
6	
7	If `source_repo` is a remote URL (GitHub URL or owner/repo format) AND tier is Forge or Deep:
8	
9	1. **Check `git` availability:** Verify `git` is functional (`git --version`). If `git` is not available, skip to the fallback warning below.
10	
11	2. **Ephemeral shallow clone:** Clone the repository to a system temp path for AST access:
12	
13	   ```
14	   temp_path = {system_temp}/skf-ephemeral-{skill-name}-{timestamp}/
15	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
16	   ```
17	
18	   **If `include_patterns` are NOT specified:**
19	
20	   ```
21	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none {source_repo} {temp_path}
22	   ```
23	
24	   **If `include_patterns` ARE specified**, use sparse-checkout to limit the clone scope:
25	
26	   ```
27	   git clone --depth 1 --branch {branch} --single-branch --filter=blob:none --sparse {source_repo} {temp_path}
28	   ```
29	
30	   **Mode selection:** Choose sparse-checkout mode based on whether `exclude_patterns` exist:
31	
32	   - **No `exclude_patterns`:** Use default **cone mode** (faster). Convert `include_patterns` to directory roots.
33	   - **`exclude_patterns` present:** Use **`--no-cone` mode** which supports gitignore-style negation patterns (`!` prefix). This applies both include and exclude at the git level, avoiding unnecessary blob downloads.
34	
35	   **Cone mode (no exclude patterns):**
36	
37	   **IMPORTANT:** `git sparse-checkout set` expects **directories**, not glob patterns. Convert `include_patterns` before passing them:
38	
39	   **Classification rule:** A pattern is an **individual file** if it contains no glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) AND does not end with `/`. Everything else is a glob — strip it to its directory root (the path prefix before the first glob character or wildcard segment).
40	
41	   - Strip glob suffixes to directory roots (e.g., `src/core/**/*.py` → `src/core`, `src/api/*.ts` → `src/api`)
42	   - Deduplicate the resulting directory list
43	   - Individual files (e.g., `pyproject.toml`, `src/utils/helpers.py`) are kept as-is
44	
45	   **If only directory roots (no individual files):**
46	
47	   ```
48	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set {converted_directory_roots}
49	   ```
50	
51	   **If any individual files are present (or mixed):**
52	
53	   ```
54	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {converted_directory_roots} {individual_files}
55	   ```
56	
57	   Example transformation:
58	   ```
59	   Brief include_patterns:        sparse-checkout args:
60	   src/core/**/*.py          →    src/core          (directory root)
61	   src/api/*.ts              →    src/api           (directory root)
62	   examples/**/*.py          →    examples          (directory root)
63	   pyproject.toml            →    pyproject.toml    (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
64	   src/utils/helpers.py      →    src/utils/helpers.py (individual file, needs --skip-checks)
65	   ```
66	
67	   **No-cone mode (exclude patterns present):**
68	
69	   When `exclude_patterns` exist, use `--no-cone` mode to pass both include and exclude patterns directly as gitignore-style rules:
70	
71	   1. Convert `include_patterns` to gitignore-style patterns. For patterns without glob characters (`*`, `?`, `[`) that do not end with `/`, apply the **file-detection heuristic**: if the last path segment contains a `.` (file extension), it is an **individual file** — prepend `/` and keep as-is (e.g., `packages/registry/registry.json` → `/packages/registry/registry.json`). If the last path segment has no extension, it is a **bare directory name** — append `/**` (e.g., `cognee` → `cognee/**`). Patterns that already contain glob characters are kept as-is (e.g., `cognee/**` → kept as-is).
72	   2. Convert `exclude_patterns` to negation patterns by prepending `!`. Apply the same file-detection heuristic: individual files (last segment has `.` extension) get `!/` prefix only (e.g., `src/internal/config.json` → `!/src/internal/config.json`); bare directory names get `/**` appended (e.g., `cognee/tests` → `!cognee/tests/**`). Patterns with globs are kept as-is with `!` prefix (e.g., `cognee/tests/**` → `!cognee/tests/**`; `**/test_*` → `!**/test_*`).
73	   3. **CRITICAL:** List all include patterns BEFORE negated exclude patterns — git processes patterns in order and a negation can only suppress a prior inclusion.
74	   4. Pass to sparse-checkout — include patterns first, then negated exclude patterns:
75	
76	   ```
77	   git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --no-cone {include_gitignore_patterns} {negated_exclude_patterns}
78	   ```
79	
80	   Example transformation:
81	   ```
82	   Brief include_patterns:          Brief exclude_patterns:
83	   cognee/**                        cognee/tests/**
84	   packages/registry/registry.json  cognee/alembic/**
85	                                    **/test_*
86	
87	   sparse-checkout args (--no-cone):
88	   'cognee/**' '/packages/registry/registry.json' '!cognee/tests/**' '!cognee/alembic/**' '!**/test_*'
89	   ```
90	
91	   Note: `registry.json` is an individual file (has `.json` extension), so it gets `/` prefix instead of `/**` suffix.
92	
93	   **Note:** `--no-cone` mode is slower than cone mode for very large repositories but eliminates downloading excluded blobs entirely.
94	
95	   **Always-included root files:**
96	
97	   Regardless of `include_patterns`, always add these root-level version/manifest files to the sparse-checkout pattern list. These are needed for version reconciliation and must not require a fallback to `gh api`:
98	
99	   `pyproject.toml`, `package.json`, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`, `setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `VERSION`
100	
101	   In cone mode, always use the `--skip-checks` command form when adding these files — even if `include_patterns` resolved to only directory roots (which would normally use the form without `--skip-checks`). The command becomes: `git -C {temp_path} sparse-checkout set --skip-checks {directory_roots} pyproject.toml package.json Cargo.toml go.mod setup.py setup.cfg VERSION`. In no-cone mode, list them as explicit include patterns before any negation patterns. Do not flag them as extraneous inclusions during post-checkout filtering.
102	
103	   **Post-checkout filtering:**
104	
105	   After checkout, apply the original glob `include_patterns` as file-level filters when building the extraction file list — sparse-checkout gets the right directories, glob filtering narrows to the exact files. When `--no-cone` mode was used, most exclude filtering is already done at the git level, but apply `exclude_patterns` as a final pass to catch any edge cases where gitignore pattern matching diverges from the brief's glob semantics. Always-included root files (see above) are exempt from post-checkout filtering.
106	
107	3. **If clone succeeds:** Update the working source path to `{temp_path}` for all subsequent AST operations in this step. Capture the source commit: `git -C {temp_path} rev-parse HEAD` — store as `source_commit` in context. Proceed with the **Forge/Deep Tier** extraction strategy below. Mark `ephemeral_clone_active = true` for cleanup.

109	4. **If clone fails (network error, auth failure, timeout):**

114

Run `cd {project-root}` using the **absolute path** captured at workflow start (not a relative path). This prevents `getcwd` errors when the temp directory is deleted while it is the shell's cwd — which happens if `cd {temp_path}` was used during CCC init or extraction operations.

121	3. **Log:** "Ephemeral source clone cleaned up."
122	
123	This ensures cleanup runs even if some extractions failed, as long as the step itself is still executing. **If any error halts the extraction step before Gate 2 is reached**, cleanup must still occur: reset cwd to `{project-root}` and attempt to delete `{temp_path}` before halting. Log the cleanup attempt regardless of success.

130	
131	After the source path is accessible, capture the current commit hash for provenance tracking:

134	- **Ephemeral clone (Forge/Deep):** already captured during clone (step 3 above)
135	- **Quick tier (remote, no clone):** `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{branch} --jq '.sha'`
136	
137	Store the result as `source_commit` in context. If capture fails (not a git repo, API unavailable), set `source_commit: null` — this is not an error.

141	## Version Reconciliation (all tiers, source mode only)
142	
143	**If `source_type: "docs-only"`:** skip this section — no source files exist to reconcile.
144	
145	After the source path is accessible (local path from step-01, or ephemeral clone from above), check whether the source contains a version identifier and reconcile it with `brief.version`. Look for the first matching version file in the resolved source path:
146	
147	- Python: `pyproject.toml` (`[project] version`), `setup.py` (`version=`), `__version__` in `__init__.py`
148	- JavaScript/TypeScript: `package.json` (`"version"`). **Monorepo resolution:** When multiple `package.json` files exist (workspace root + packages), resolve version using this priority:
149	  1. Package whose `name` field matches `brief.name` (e.g., the skill's target library name)
150	  2. Package with a `bin` field (CLI entry point — represents the published version)
151	  3. Root workspace `package.json` version (if present)
152	  4. Fall back to `brief.version` if no version found. For monorepos using workspace protocols (pnpm, yarn, npm workspaces), the root `package.json` often has no `version` field — this is expected, not an error.
153	- Rust: `Cargo.toml` (`[package] version`)
154	- Go: `go.mod` (module version if tagged)
155	
156	**If a source version is found AND it differs from `brief.version`:**
157	
158	⚠️ Warn the user: "Brief version ({brief.version}) differs from source version ({source_version}). Using source version ({source_version})."
159	
160	Update the working version in context to the source version. Record the mismatch in context for the evidence report (step-08).
161	
162	**If no version file is found or version cannot be extracted:** keep `brief.version` as-is. No warning needed.
163	
164	**If source is remote and accessed via Quick tier (gh_bridge, no local files):** attempt to read the version file via `gh_bridge.read_file(owner, repo, "{version_file}")` — resolved as `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/contents/{version_file}` or direct file read if local (see [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md)) — for the primary version file of the detected language. If the read fails, keep `brief.version`.

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Here's what I found:

**No — brief exclusion patterns are NOT applied to CCC settings before indexing the ephemeral clone.**

The deferred CCC discovery in step-03-extract.md (lines 85-96) does:
1. `ccc init` on the ephemeral clone
2. `ccc index` on the ephemeral clone
3. Constructs semantic query from brief data
4. Executes search

But there's no step between `ccc init` and `ccc index` that configures exclusion patterns in the clone's `.cocoindex_code/settings.yml`.

Compare this with **setup-forge step-01b** (for local repos), which explicitly:
- Builds SKF exclusion patterns (section 2b, lines 81-118)
- Applies them to `settings.yml` BEFORE running `ccc index` (section 3, lines 137-144)
- Even has a failure metric: "Indexing without configuring SKF exclusion patterns in `.cocoindex_code/settings.yml`" (line 199)

So there are **two gaps** in the deferred discovery path:
1. **SKF infrastructure exclusions** — not applied (though less relevant for ephemeral clones of remote repos since `_bmad`, `_bmad-output`, etc. wouldn't typically exist in the source repo)
2. **Brief exclusion patterns** — never applied to CCC anywhere in the workflow. They're used for sparse-checkout (source-resolution-protocols), scope filtering (step-03 section 2), and AST CLI streaming EXCLUDES (section 4), but CCC indexes everything and searches everything

This means CCC search results for remote repos can waste `top_k=20` slots on files the brief explicitly excludes.

## Implementation Plan: Brief Exclusion Pattern Support for CCC Indexing of Ephemeral Clones

### Problem Statement

When the create-skill workflow clones a remote repo ephemerally and runs deferred CCC indexing on it (step-03-extract.md, section 2a), the brief's `exclude_patterns` are never applied to the clone's `.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` before `ccc index` runs. This means:

1. CCC wastes time indexing files the brief explicitly excludes (e.g., `tests/**`, `docs/**`, `internal/**`)
2. CCC search results may contain irrelevant files from excluded directories, polluting the `{ccc_discovery}` results that pre-rank the extraction queue

The local-repo path (setup-forge step-01b) already applies SKF infrastructure exclusions to settings.yml before indexing. The ephemeral clone path needs analogous treatment for brief-level exclusions.

### Architecture Context

There are two separate exclusion concerns:

1. **SKF infrastructure exclusions** (setup-forge step-01b): `**/_bmad`, `**/_bmad-output`, etc. These apply to the local project repo and are irrelevant for ephemeral clones of external repos (the cloned repo will not contain `_bmad` directories).

2. **Brief exclusion patterns** (this change): `tests/**`, `**/test_*`, `docs/**`, etc. These are user-specified in the brief and apply to the target source code being analyzed. They are already used by sparse-checkout (source-resolution-protocols.md, no-cone mode) and by the AST extraction streaming pipeline (extraction-patterns.md, `EXCLUDES` list). But they are NOT currently propagated to CCC's settings.yml for the ephemeral clone.

### Format Mapping Analysis

**Brief `exclude_patterns` format examples:** `tests/**`, `**/test_*`, `docs/**`, `internal/**`, `cognee/tests/**`, `cognee/alembic/**`

**CCC `settings.yml` `exclude_patterns` format:** `**/node_modules`, `**/__pycache__`, `**/.git` (always `**/` prefixed glob patterns)

The formats partially overlap but are not identical:

- Brief patterns like `**/test_*` are already CCC-compatible (use `**/` prefix)
- Brief patterns like `tests/**` are directory-rooted relative patterns. CCC's convention uses `**/` prefix to match anywhere in the tree. For an ephemeral clone (where the clone root IS the repo root), `tests/**` would match correctly as-is in most glob engines, but for consistency with CCC convention, patterns without `**/` prefix could optionally be kept as-is since they are valid globs
- The key insight: CCC settings.yml `exclude_patterns` accepts standard glob patterns. Brief patterns are already glob-compatible. No complex conversion is needed -- the patterns can be appended directly. If a pattern like `tests/**` only matches at the root, that is correct behavior for the ephemeral clone (which IS rooted at the repo root).

If init fails or indexing fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and continue — this is not an error.
```

Wait -- let me re-examine. The old steps are 1 through 5. With the split, the new sequence is 1 through 7. Let me lay this out precisely.

**Full replacement for lines 87-96 (the numbered steps under "Then run CCC indexing and discovery on the ephemeral clone:"):**

```
1. **Initialize index:** Run `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` — `ccc init` takes no positional arguments and initializes the index for the current working directory. If init fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and continue — this is not an error.

2. **Apply brief exclusions:** If `brief.exclude_patterns` is present and non-empty, apply them to `{temp_path}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` before indexing:
   1. Read `{temp_path}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` (created by `ccc init`)
   2. For each pattern in `brief.exclude_patterns`: if the pattern is NOT already present in the `exclude_patterns` array, append it
   3. Write the updated `settings.yml` back
   This prevents CCC from indexing excluded files, keeping search results focused on in-scope source code. If `brief.exclude_patterns` is absent or empty, skip this step.

3. **Index the clone:** Run `cd {temp_path} && ccc index` with an extended timeout or in background mode. Indexing can take several minutes on large codebases (1000+ files). Use `ccc status` to verify completion — check that `Chunks` and `Files` counts are non-zero. If indexing fails, set `{ccc_discovery: []}` and continue — this is not an error.

4. **Construct semantic query:** Build from brief data: `"{brief.name} {brief.scope}"`. Truncate to 80 characters — keep the full skill name and trim `brief.scope` from the end. If `brief.scope` is very short (< 10 chars), append terms from `brief.description` to fill the remaining space.

5. **Execute search:** Run `ccc_bridge.search(query, temp_path, top_k=20)`:
   - **Tool resolution:** Use `/ccc` skill search (Claude Code), ccc MCP server (Cursor), or `cd {temp_path} && ccc search --limit 20 "{query}"` (CLI). Note: `ccc search` operates on the index in the current working directory. See [knowledge/tool-resolution.md](../../../knowledge/tool-resolution.md).

6. **Store results:** If search succeeds, store as `{ccc_discovery: [{file, score, snippet}]}`. Display: "**CCC semantic discovery (post-clone): {N} relevant regions identified across {M} unique files.**"

7. **On failure:** Set `{ccc_discovery: []}`. Display: "CCC post-clone discovery unavailable — proceeding with standard extraction." Do NOT halt.
```

#### File 2: `src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md`

**Location:** Lines 99-108, the "Deferred Discovery (Remote Sources)" section.

**Current content (lines 99-108):**
```markdown
### Deferred Discovery (Remote Sources)

For remote repository sources (GitHub URLs), CCC cannot operate during step-02b because no local code exists yet. The ephemeral clone happens in step-03. To provide CCC pre-ranking for remote sources:

1. **step-02b:** Detects remote source, sets `{ccc_discovery: []}`, displays deferred message
2. **step-03:** After ephemeral clone succeeds, detects the deferred scenario (`tools.ccc == true AND {ccc_discovery} is empty AND ephemeral_clone_active == true AND tier is Forge+/Deep`)
3. **step-03:** Runs `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` + `ccc index` on the ephemeral clone (extended timeout or background mode, verified via `ccc status`)
4. **step-03:** Executes CCC search and populates `{ccc_discovery}` before AST extraction begins

The ephemeral clone index is not registered in `ccc_index_registry` — the clone is deleted after extraction and the CCC daemon's own GC handles orphaned indexes.
```

**Change:** Update step 3 to mention that brief exclusion patterns are applied between `ccc init` and `ccc index`. This keeps the knowledge doc aligned with the workflow step.

**New content for lines 99-108:**
```markdown
### Deferred Discovery (Remote Sources)

For remote repository sources (GitHub URLs), CCC cannot operate during step-02b because no local code exists yet. The ephemeral clone happens in step-03. To provide CCC pre-ranking for remote sources:

1. **step-02b:** Detects remote source, sets `{ccc_discovery: []}`, displays deferred message
2. **step-03:** After ephemeral clone succeeds, detects the deferred scenario (`tools.ccc == true AND {ccc_discovery} is empty AND ephemeral_clone_active == true AND tier is Forge+/Deep`)
3. **step-03:** Runs `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` on the ephemeral clone, then applies brief `exclude_patterns` (if present) to `{temp_path}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` before running `ccc index` (extended timeout or background mode, verified via `ccc status`). This mirrors how setup-forge step-01b applies SKF exclusions before indexing local repos — brief exclusions prevent CCC from indexing files the user explicitly excluded, keeping search results focused on in-scope source code.
4. **step-03:** Executes CCC search and populates `{ccc_discovery}` before AST extraction begins

The ephemeral clone index is not registered in `ccc_index_registry` — the clone is deleted after extraction and the CCC daemon's own GC handles orphaned indexes.
```

### Design Decisions and Rationale

**1. No format conversion needed:** Brief `exclude_patterns` are standard glob patterns. CCC's `settings.yml` accepts standard glob patterns. While CCC's default patterns tend to use `**/` prefix convention, patterns like `tests/**` or `docs/**` are valid globs that will match correctly when the CCC index root is the repo root (which it is for ephemeral clones). Adding a normalization step would be unnecessary complexity and could break patterns like `cognee/tests/**` (a path-rooted pattern that should NOT be prefixed with `**/`).

**2. Append-only, deduplication check:** Following the same pattern as step-01b-ccc-index.md (lines 108): "if the pattern is NOT already present in `exclude_patterns`, append it." This preserves CCC's default exclusions (node_modules, __pycache__, etc.) and only adds brief-specific ones.

**3. No SKF infrastructure exclusions for ephemeral clones:** The ephemeral clone is of an external repository that does not contain `_bmad`, `_bmad-output`, `.claude`, etc. Applying SKF exclusions to it would be harmless but pointless. The change only applies brief exclusions.

**4. Init failure handling stays at step 1:** If `ccc init` fails, we skip straight to `{ccc_discovery: []}`. There is no point trying to apply exclusions to a settings.yml that does not exist. The new step 2 only runs if init succeeded (settings.yml exists).

**5. Step renumbering:** The old step 1 combined init + index. Splitting them into separate numbered steps (1: init, 2: exclusions, 3: index) makes the sequence explicit and matches the three-phase pattern used in step-01b-ccc-index.md (init, configure exclusions, index).

### Potential Edge Cases

- **Brief has no `exclude_patterns`:** Step 2 is skipped entirely. Behavior is identical to current behavior.
- **`ccc init` fails:** `{ccc_discovery: []}` is set at step 1 and remaining steps are skipped. Step 2 never tries to read a non-existent settings.yml.
- **settings.yml does not have an `exclude_patterns` key:** `ccc init` creates settings.yml with defaults that include an `exclude_patterns` array. If for some reason the key is missing, the implementer should create it.
- **Brief patterns overlap with CCC defaults:** The deduplication check ("if NOT already present") handles this cleanly.
- **Sparse-checkout already filtered files at git level:** Yes, when `--no-cone` mode was used with `exclude_patterns`, the excluded files were never downloaded. But CCC operates on the filesystem tree, and some patterns may not perfectly align between gitignore-style sparse-checkout and CCC's glob matching. Applying exclusions to CCC settings is belt-and-suspenders: it ensures CCC's own index respects the exclusions even if sparse-checkout was not used (e.g., when `include_patterns` is absent, no sparse-checkout happens but `exclude_patterns` may still be present).

### Implementation Sequence

1. **Edit `step-03-extract.md`** (primary): Replace lines 87-96 with the 7-step sequence described above.
2. **Edit `ccc-bridge.md`** (secondary): Replace lines 105-106 with the updated step 3 text, and add the new anti-pattern bullet after line 133.
3. **Verify** no other files reference the old step numbering within the deferred discovery section (they do not -- the step numbers are local to this numbered list).

### Critical Files for Implementation

- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` -- primary edit target, lines 87-96
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md` -- secondary edit target, lines 105-106 and line 133
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/setup-forge/steps-c/step-01b-ccc-index.md` -- reference pattern for exclusion application (lines 101-114), not modified
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/source-resolution-protocols.md` -- context for sparse-checkout exclusion handling, not modified
- `/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/workflows/create-skill/data/extraction-patterns.md` -- context for AST streaming EXCLUDES pattern, not modified
```

# Plan: Apply Brief Exclusion Patterns to CCC Before Indexing Ephemeral Clones

## Context

During create-skill for remote repos, the deferred CCC discovery path (step-03-extract.md, section 2a) runs `ccc init` + `ccc index` on the ephemeral clone but never applies the brief's `exclude_patterns` to `.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` before indexing. This means CCC wastes time indexing excluded files and search results can waste `top_k=20` slots on irrelevant files. For very large repos (e.g., Storybook), this extra indexing work exacerbates timeout failures.

By contrast, setup-forge step-01b explicitly applies SKF infrastructure exclusions before `ccc index` for local repos.

## Changes

### 1. `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` (primary)

**Section 2a, "Deferred CCC Discovery"** — split old step 1 (init + index) into 3 steps, renumber the rest:

1. **Initialize index:** `ccc init` only. If init fails → `{ccc_discovery: []}`, skip remaining steps.
2. **Apply brief exclusions (new):** If `brief.exclude_patterns` is non-empty:
   - Read `{temp_path}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml`
   - Append each pattern not already present in `exclude_patterns`
   - Write back. Skip if no exclude_patterns in brief.
3. **Index the clone:** `ccc index` with extended timeout. Verify via `ccc status`.
4-7. Renumbered from old steps 2-5 (no content changes).

### 2. `src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md` (secondary)

- **"Deferred Discovery" section (line ~105):** Update step 3 to document that brief `exclude_patterns` are applied between `ccc init` and `ccc index`.
- **"Anti-Patterns" section (after line 133):** Add: "Indexing an ephemeral clone without applying brief `exclude_patterns`"

## Design Decisions

- **No format conversion needed** — brief patterns are standard globs, CCC settings.yml accepts standard globs. Patterns like `tests/**` work correctly when the index root is the repo root.
- **Append-only with dedup** — matches step-01b pattern. Preserves CCC defaults (node_modules, etc.).
- **No SKF infra exclusions for clones** — the external repo won't have `_bmad` etc. directories.
- **Init failure short-circuits** — if `ccc init` fails, step 2 never tries to read a non-existent settings.yml.

## Files

|------|--------|
| `src/workflows/create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` | Edit lines 87-96 |
| `src/knowledge/ccc-bridge.md` | Edit lines 105-106, add anti-pattern after line 133 |

## Verification

- Read both files after editing to confirm coherence
- Verify the step numbering in step-03-extract.md is consistent (1-7)
- Verify ccc-bridge.md "Deferred Discovery" section matches step-03's updated flow

99	### Deferred Discovery (Remote Sources)
100	
101	For remote repository sources (GitHub URLs), CCC cannot operate during step-02b because no local code exists yet. The ephemeral clone happens in step-03. To provide CCC pre-ranking for remote sources:
102	
103	1. **step-02b:** Detects remote source, sets `{ccc_discovery: []}`, displays deferred message
104	2. **step-03:** After ephemeral clone succeeds, detects the deferred scenario (`tools.ccc == true AND {ccc_discovery} is empty AND ephemeral_clone_active == true AND tier is Forge+/Deep`)
105	3. **step-03:** Runs `cd {temp_path} && ccc init` on the ephemeral clone, then applies brief `exclude_patterns` (if present) to `{temp_path}/.cocoindex_code/settings.yml` before running `ccc index` (extended timeout or background mode, verified via `ccc status`). This prevents CCC from indexing files the brief explicitly excludes, keeping search results focused on in-scope source code and reducing indexing time for large repositories.
106	4. **step-03:** Executes CCC search and populates `{ccc_discovery}` before AST extraction begins
107	
108	The ephemeral clone index is not registered in `ccc_index_registry` — the clone is deleted after extraction and the CCC daemon's own GC handles orphaned indexes.
109	
110	### Relationship to QMD Registry
111	
112	ccc_index and qmd_collections are **orthogonal**:
113	- `ccc_index` in forge-tier.yaml tracks the persistent source code index (one per project)
114	- `qmd_collections[]` in forge-tier.yaml tracks per-skill workflow artifact collections
115	- ccc indexes source code for semantic search; QMD indexes curated artifacts for temporal/knowledge search
116	- The janitor role for QMD (setup-forge step-03) operates independently of ccc_index
117	
118	## Query Volume Bounds
119	
120	To prevent excessive daemon calls, workflow steps cap ccc queries:
121	- **create-skill extraction:** max 2 queries per skill (discovery + optional scope refinement)
122	- **analyze-source mapping:** max 1 query per qualifying unit
123	- **create-stack-skill integration detection:** max 1 query per library pair
124	- **audit-skill re-index:** max 1 query per export missing from its recorded location
125	
126	## Anti-Patterns
127	
128	- Using ccc_bridge results as citations without ast-grep verification — ccc output is never a provenance citation
129	- Blocking a workflow because ccc is unavailable — ccc is always optional
130	- Running ccc_bridge.ensure_index() without checking ccc_index.status first — unnecessary re-indexing
131	- Passing ccc results directly to the extraction inventory — they are candidates, not extractions
132	- Listing ccc as "unavailable" in reports for Quick/Forge tiers — ccc is a Forge+ capability, not something Quick/Forge tiers are missing
133	- Indexing without configuring SKF exclusions — framework and output directories pollute search results
134	- Indexing an ephemeral clone without applying brief `exclude_patterns` — excluded files pollute CCC search results and waste indexing time on large repositories
135	- Skipping CCC discovery for remote sources without deferring to step-03 — remote repos deserve the same pre-ranking as local sources
136	
137	## Related Fragments
138	
139	- [tool-resolution.md](tool-resolution.md) — canonical bridge-to-tool and subprocess-to-tool mapping per IDE
140	- [progressive-capability.md](progressive-capability.md) — Forge+ tier definition and positive framing
141	- [confidence-tiers.md](confidence-tiers.md) — why ccc does not create a new confidence tier
142	- [qmd-registry.md](qmd-registry.md) — the parallel but separate registry for QMD collections
143
